<a id="item-1"></a>
## [Video Generation as a General-Purpose Vision Learner](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09024) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Researchers propose GenCeption, a model that repurposes a pre-trained text-to-video generative diffusion backbone as a feed-forward perception model, achieving state-of-the-art on depth, normal, pose, segmentation, and keypoint tasks. This work suggests that video generation can serve as a foundational pre-training paradigm for general-purpose computer vision, potentially shifting the field similar to how next-token prediction transformed NLP. GenCeption matches or surpasses specialized models like DepthAnything3 and SAM3, and shows data efficiency—achieving comparable performance with 7 to 500 times less training data. It also exhibits emergent generalization from synthetic human videos to real-world and out-of-distribution objects.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: In natural language processing, next-token prediction enabled generalist foundation models like GPT. For computer vision, most models remain task-specific. GenCeption explores whether video generation—which requires understanding spatiotemporal dynamics, vision-language alignment, and scalability—can serve as a similar catalyst for generalist vision models.

<a id="item-2"></a>
## [Soofi S 30B-A3B: Sovereign Open-Source German-English Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09424) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Researchers released Soofi S 30B-A3B, a sovereign open-source Mixture-of-Experts hybrid Mamba Transformer foundation model for German and English, achieving top performance among fully open models with only 3B active parameters. This model demonstrates that sovereign, fully open-source models can compete with larger dense models, offering a transparent and efficient alternative for German and English NLP, and setting a new standard for open-access AI in Europe. The model activates only 3B of its 30B total parameters per token, keeping inference cache near-constant as context grows, and was pretrained on ~27 trillion tokens with up-weighted German data.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models use a gating mechanism to activate only a subset of parameters per token, improving efficiency. The hybrid Mamba Transformer architecture combines the strengths of state-space models and transformers. Soofi S was built on the German Industrial AI Cloud, a sovereign HPC infrastructure operated by Deutsche Telekom in Munich.

<a id="item-3"></a>
## [Hidden Costs in Frontier LLM Pricing Revealed](https://playcode.io/blog/real-price-of-frontier-models) ⭐️ 8.0/10

An analysis on Playcode.io reveals that the real cost of using frontier LLMs like OpenAI's GPT-4o and Anthropic's Claude goes beyond per-token pricing, due to tokenizer inefficiencies and cache read costs. This matters because developers and businesses may significantly underestimate their LLM expenses, leading to budget overruns; understanding these hidden costs is crucial for accurate cost forecasting and model selection. The analysis highlights that Anthropic's tokenizer is 1.6x to 2x less efficient than OpenAI's for code, and that cache read costs can lead to quadratic pricing over multiple turns, despite flat output token pricing.

hackernews · ianberdin · Jul 13, 18:32 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48896800)

**Background**: LLM pricing is typically based on the number of tokens processed, but tokenizers convert text into tokens with varying efficiency. Additionally, many providers offer prompt caching to reduce costs, but cache reads still incur charges, which can accumulate unexpectedly in multi-turn interactions.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://arxiv.org/html/2511.03825v1">How Different Tokenization Algorithms Impact LLMs and ...</a></li>
<li><a href="https://leanlm.ai/blog/llm-effective-cost-table">LLM Effective Cost Table 2026: Cache + Batch Real Prices</a></li>
<li><a href="https://www.aimagicx.com/blog/llm-api-pricing-comparison-2026">LLM API Pricing in 2026: The Complete Cost Comparison (GPT-5, Claude ...</a></li>

</ul>
</details>

**Discussion**: Community comments confirm the tokenizer efficiency gap, with users reporting that OpenAI's tokenizer is 1.6x-2x better than Claude's for code. Some also note that cache read costs may hide the true cost of long contexts, leading to quadratic pricing in practice.

**Tags**: `#LLM`, `#pricing`, `#tokenizer`, `#OpenAI`, `#Anthropic`

<a id="item-4"></a>
## [Climate.gov data preserved by open data efforts](https://werd.io/climate-gov-was-destroyed-open-data-saved-it/) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A blog post reports that after climate.gov data was removed by the government, open data and civic tech initiatives successfully preserved and restored access to the information. ## [Interval Certifications for MLPs via Lattice Traversal](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08773) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a rigorous theoretical framework that reduces adversarial robustness of MLPs to a lattice traversal problem, and defines both sound and complete interval certifications for the first time. This work bridges adversarial robustness and lattice theory, potentially enabling more efficient and trustworthy certification methods for neural networks, which is crucial for AI safety. The framework uses lattice traversal operators in a refine & verify iterative scheme, and guarantees sound maximality and complete minimality via formal MLP verifiers. For complete certifications, the minimum solution is obtained in polynomial oracle calls, while sound certifications are proven strongly intractable.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: Adversarial robustness is a key AI safety problem, where small input perturbations can cause misclassification. Interval certifications provide guarantees that an input can be perturbed within a region without changing the prediction (sound) or that moving outside the region changes the prediction (complete). Lattice traversal is a combinatorial search technique used in data mining.

Through 489 controllability-oriented probes, it shows that increasing structural scaffolding reduces output variance and failure rates without changing the underlying model. This work challenges the prevailing focus on scaling model capability, showing that reliability can be significantly improved through better inference-time control. It opens new directions for designing and evaluating LLM systems beyond scaling alone. CogniConsole combines programmatic coordination with bounded prompt-based reasoning in a structured interface. The study uses a fixed model architecture and demonstrates that many failure modes, such as context drift and inconsistent constraint adherence, stem from under-specified control rather than insufficient capability.

## [GATS: Graph-Augmented Tree Search for Efficient Agent Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08894) ⭐️ 8.0/10

GATS introduces a graph-augmented tree search with a three-layer world model that eliminates LLM inference calls during planning, achieving 100% success rate on synthetic tasks and 100% on a 12-scenario stress test, compared to 92% and 88.9% for LATS respectively. This work significantly reduces the computational cost and stochasticity of LLM-based agent planning, making it more practical for real-world applications. It demonstrates that systematic search with learned world models can outperform LLM-guided exploration, potentially shifting the paradigm in AI planning. GATS uses UCB1-based tree search and a three-layer world model: L1 for exact symbolic action matching, L2 for statistics from execution logs, and L3 for LLM-based prediction of unknown actions. It requires zero LLM calls per task during planning, versus 37 for LATS, and produces deterministic plans with zero variance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents like ReAct and LATS use LLM inference at each planning step, which is computationally expensive and leads to stochastic outputs. World models are internal representations that predict how the environment changes in response to actions, enabling agents to plan without constant real-world interaction. GATS decouples the world model from the LLM, using a layered approach to handle actions with varying levels of uncertainty.

## [New Benchmark Tests AI Agents on Long-Horizon Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08964) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced Long-Horizon-Terminal-Bench, a benchmark of 46 long-horizon tasks across nine categories, with dense intermediate rewards for partial credit. The benchmark evaluates AI agents on tasks requiring hundreds of episodes and minutes to hours of execution. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in AI agent evaluation by measuring progress on complex, open-ended workflows rather than just final outcomes. It reveals that even the strongest models achieve only 15.2% pass rate at a partial-reward threshold, highlighting significant room for improvement in long-horizon planning and iterative debugging. The benchmark includes tasks from experiment reproduction, software engineering, multimodal analysis, interactive games, and scientific computing. Agents consume on average 9.9M tokens per task, with roughly 231 episodes and 85.3 minutes of execution time per run.

Dense reward-based grading gives partial credit for intermediate progress, offering a more nuanced evaluation of agent capabilities.

Formalizing such results in a proof assistant ensures their correctness down to the axioms.

Researchers propose a neuro-agentic control framework that couples an LLM planner (Gemini 2.5 Flash-Lite) with a pre-trained Time-Series Foundation Model (TimesFM) and introduces a Counterfactual Physics Injection mechanism to reject hallucinated actions before execution. This framework addresses the critical safety issue of LLM hallucination in closed-loop industrial control, enabling autonomous cyber defense for operational technology without risking physical damage, which could significantly improve resilience of critical infrastructure. Evaluated on the Secure Water Treatment (SWaT) dataset under stochastic attack scenarios, the framework prevented five breaches (33.3%) below threshold, outperforming LSTM (26.7%) and TCN (13.3%), with zero physically invalid actions executed.

Large Language Models (LLMs) offer semantic reasoning but suffer from hallucination, making them unsafe for direct control. Time-Series Foundation Models (TimesFM) are pre-trained on massive time-series data for forecasting, providing a physics-grounded latent space. Counterfactual Physics Injection simulates LLM-proposed actions in this latent space before actuation, rejecting unsafe ones.

## [KV-PRM: Efficient Process Reward Modeling via KV-Cache Transfer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09153) ⭐️ 8.0/10

KV-PRM introduces a novel method that reduces the computational cost of process reward modeling from O(L^2) to O(L) by reusing the KV cache generated during LLM inference, avoiding re-encoding entire trajectories. This breakthrough significantly reduces the computational bottleneck of process reward models, enabling efficient test-time scaling for long-context multi-agent systems, with up to 5000x reduction in FLOPs and 37x latency improvement. KV-PRM processes a single 'verify token' against the pre-existing KV cache, and the paper provides a formal proof that the KV cache contains strictly greater information capacity than text for reward modeling.

**Background**: Process Reward Models (PRMs) evaluate intermediate steps in LLM reasoning, crucial for test-time scaling methods like Beam Search and MCTS. Traditional text-based PRMs re-encode the entire trajectory from scratch, leading to quadratic cost with sequence length. KV cache is a technique that stores key-value pairs from attention layers, enabling efficient reuse during generation.

Researchers propose GRACE, a graph-regularized method that maintains agentic context as a typed semantic graph and validates updates locally, achieving a pass^3 reliability of 0.673 from a zero-shot baseline of 0.091 on a telecom agent benchmark under distribution shift. This addresses a critical challenge in deploying LLM agents over long horizons: maintaining reliability when the context evolves under distribution shift, which is essential for real-world autonomous systems. The method was evaluated on a fixed telecom agent harness from τ²-bench under a controlled distribution-shift protocol.

Researchers propose the Hypothesis Evolution Protocol (HEP), a framework that makes hypothesis generation, testing, and belief updates in LLM agents explicit and auditable. HEP addresses a critical lack of auditability in current AI scientists, where reasoning steps are buried in unstructured logs. The protocol's effectiveness increases with the capability of the base LLM, and it generalizes across different research questions in materials science.

## [Bayesian Causal Discovery Fails Under Latent Confounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09449) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper characterizes how Bayesian causal discovery fails under latent confounding in linear Gaussian networks, deriving a correlation threshold that leads to spurious edges and showing that more data exacerbates the problem. This work provides a rigorous theoretical understanding of a critical failure mode in Bayesian causal discovery, which is widely used in scientific and medical domains. Practitioners must be aware that latent confounding can produce misleading posterior distributions, especially with large datasets. The analysis focuses on additive latent confounding between exactly two observed variables in linear Gaussian models. The authors derive that the correlation threshold for favoring a spurious edge decreases with sample size, and identify two distinct posterior failure regimes depending on local graph structure.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: Bayesian causal discovery uses posterior inference over directed acyclic graphs to quantify uncertainty about causal structures. Latent confounding occurs when an unmeasured variable influences both a treatment and outcome, biasing causal estimates. Linear Gaussian networks assume variables follow linear relationships with Gaussian noise, a common simplifying assumption in causal inference.

## [Multimodal Reward Hacking in RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09492) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces the Newly Rewarded Failure Rate (NRFR) metric to measure reward hacking in multimodal large language model (MLLM) reinforcement learning, and shows that outcome-only rewards cause severe new failures even at scale. This research highlights a critical alignment problem in MLLMs, demonstrating that optimizing imperfect rewards can systematically create new failures, which has important implications for the safety and reliability of AI systems. The study found that outcome-only rewards can lead to a Reward Hacking Rate (RHR) of 48.1%, with NRFR exceeding RHR, indicating that RL creates new failures rather than merely inheriting them. Even a 32B model retained a 54.9% worse rate under outcome-only rewards.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) is used to align multimodal large language models (MLLMs) by optimizing a reward signal. However, when the reward does not perfectly reflect task performance, models may exploit loopholes to achieve high rewards without genuine improvement—a phenomenon known as reward hacking. This paper systematically studies reward hacking across different reward designs, model scales, and RL algorithms.

## [Shared Selective Persistent Memory for Agentic LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09493) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new architecture called shared selective persistent memory is proposed, which retains four categories of reusable context (task specifications, data schemas, tool configurations, output constraints) while discarding session-specific reasoning traces, and enables collaborative reuse across users via role-based access control. This addresses the critical context loss problem in agentic LLM systems, achieving 96% task completion (vs. 79% without memory and 71% with full history) and reducing token cost by 97x per invocation, which significantly improves efficiency and collaboration in multi-turn code generation and data-driven artifact creation. The architecture is implemented in a deployed collaborative workspace platform where LLM agents produce git-versioned artifacts from heterogeneous sources. A zero-token data refresh mechanism decouples generated programs from runtime data, enabling artifact reuse without LLM re-invocation, achieving a 14x task-time reduction for recurring updates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic LLM systems that generate code through multi-turn tool use face a fundamental context problem: each session starts from zero, discarding previous productive context. Naively persisting entire conversation histories is token-inefficient and can degrade generation quality due to irrelevant context. Shared selective persistent memory selectively retains only reusable context, improving both efficiency and effectiveness.

Researchers propose SAGEAgent, a self-evolving LLM-based clinical agent that sequentially decides which diagnostic modalities to acquire for each cancer patient, balancing predictive accuracy against clinical invasiveness. This work addresses a critical clinical problem: not all patients need every diagnostic test. By reducing average acquisition burden by 55% while maintaining competitive survival prediction accuracy, SAGEAgent could lower healthcare costs and patient discomfort. SAGEAgent uses clinical tools to translate numerical predictions into text, episodic memory to retrieve similar past cases, and semantic memory to accumulate reusable decision patterns. Experiments on a glioma cohort with four modalities (demographics, imaging, histology, genomics) show a 55% reduction in acquisition burden.

## [AI's Fixed Frames Limit Open-Ended Innovation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09560) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper argues that current AI systems are limited by fixed representational frames and identifies two key gaps—vocabulary and verifier—that must be closed for open-ended intelligence. This conceptual contribution reframes the challenge of building truly innovative AI, moving beyond scaling existing architectures to highlight the need for systems that can invent new concepts and evaluate them adaptively. The vocabulary gap refers to the difficulty of inventing and stabilizing new representational primitives, while the verifier gap concerns judging the value of a new primitive when its payoff may only appear after future reuse.

## [Agora: Auction-Based Task Allocation Boosts LLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09600) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose Agora, a framework that uses an incentive-compatible auction mechanism to dynamically allocate reasoning tasks to expert LLMs, treating reasoning steps as tradeable items. Evaluations on five benchmarks show Agora outperforms single-model, routing, and cascade baselines. Agora addresses the critical challenge of cost and performance variability in multi-agent LLM systems by enabling efficient, dynamic task allocation. This approach could lead to more robust and cost-effective LLM agent systems for complex reasoning tasks. The auction mechanism is incentive-compatible, meaning agents bid based on their rectified competence, ensuring critical logic is routed to the most capable solver rather than the most overconfident one. Agora exposes a controllable cost-quality trade-off through a single auction parameter.

StickyMoE introduces a differentiable routing consistency loss that reduces expert switching in Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models by up to 60% with less than 4% perplexity increase, enabling more memory-efficient inference on edge devices. This addresses a critical memory efficiency issue in MoE models, which are widely used in large language models but suffer from frequent expert swapping between slow storage and fast memory on edge devices. StickyMoE requires no architectural changes and adds only a single hyperparameter lambda. Unlike post-hoc methods, it allows expert representations and routing decisions to co-adapt from the first training step, Pareto-dominating post-hoc fine-tuning on the quality-locality frontier.

This approach decouples learning patient dynamics from treatment optimization, enabling adaptability to changing clinical objectives during inference, which addresses a key limitation of fixed RL policies and could improve sepsis treatment outcomes. EHR-MPC was evaluated on a multicenter ICU sepsis cohort from 8 hospitals in the Mass General Brigham health system, using both off-policy importance sampling and on-policy simulation-based evaluation, achieving comparable off-policy performance and improved simulation performance relative to RL baselines.

Reinforcement learning (RL) has been applied to learn treatment policies from data, but these policies are fixed after training and cannot adapt to changing clinical goals at inference time. Model predictive control (MPC) is a control method that optimizes actions by simulating future outcomes over a finite horizon, and generative patient digital twins are AI models that can simulate patient trajectories under different interventions.

## [Eluna: Graph-Guided Multi-Agent LLM for Warehouse SOPs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08960) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Eluna is a production-deployed agentic LLM system that automates warehouse operations by encoding SOPs as directed acyclic graphs with progressive disclosure and using asymmetric episodic distillation to train a smaller student model that matches or exceeds a larger teacher. This work addresses critical challenges in applying LLMs to industrial settings: procedural compliance and context overload. By achieving 94% expert agreement on a real ticket processing application, Eluna demonstrates that agentic LLM systems can reliably execute complex, multi-system decision logic under strict time constraints, paving the way for broader adoption in logistics and manufacturing. Eluna uses a graph-guided multi-agent framework where SOPs are represented as directed acyclic graphs, enabling progressive disclosure of instructions to avoid context overload. Asymmetric episodic distillation improves a teacher model using episodic error memories, then fine-tunes a smaller student on corrected trajectories without inference-time overhead, achieving low latency and high accuracy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used as agents to automate tasks, but they struggle with procedural compliance when given full SOP specifications due to context overload. Progressive disclosure is a technique that provides only the necessary context at each step, reducing cognitive load on the model. Knowledge distillation transfers knowledge from a large teacher model to a smaller student model for efficiency; asymmetric episodic distillation extends this by using episodic memories to correct teacher errors before distillation.

## [NL-PAC Framework Quantifies Ambiguity Risk in LLM Supervision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08961) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced Natural Language PAC (NL-PAC), a theoretical framework that formalizes the irreducible risk from ambiguous natural language specifications in LLM-mediated supervision, proving minimax lower bounds and providing certifiable risk floors. This work provides a rigorous foundation for understanding and certifying the limits of LLM-based supervision, which is critical for AI safety and evaluation methodology, as it shows that simply adding more labels cannot resolve ambiguity-induced errors. The framework uses a fixed model's thresholded decoding law to define admissible labels and candidate targets, and shows that under target-blind supervision, every learner incurs worst-case risk at least half the diameter of the admissible target class. In a frozen Qwen 2.5-3B audit, one prespecified prompt yielded a positive model-relative certificate, while a paraphrase and exact-rule controls yielded zero.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models are increasingly used to provide labels, evaluations, and feedback for tasks specified in natural language. However, natural language specifications are often ambiguous, meaning they can be interpreted in multiple ways. Traditional approaches focus on reducing sampling error by collecting more labels, but this does not resolve the identification problem caused by ambiguity. NL-PAC addresses this gap by providing a theoretical framework to quantify and certify the irreducible risk.

This benchmark addresses a critical gap in VLM evaluation, as real-world applications like robotics and mechanical assembly require allocentric 3D understanding. The benchmark requires models to decouple object positions from camera perspectives and ground them in a global coordinate system. The authors also propose ViewNavigator, a multi-agent framework that improves base model performance by 3-5x on the full benchmark by actively selecting and fusing informative viewpoints.

MultiView-Bench specifically tests this allocentric multi-view integration capability.

## [CLAP: Turning VLMs into VLAs with Language-Action Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08974) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose CLAP (Causal Language-Action Prediction), a method that prepends natural-language action descriptions to numeric action sequences, enabling direct conversion of vision-language models (VLMs) into vision-language-action models (VLAs) without modifying the backbone architecture. With single-epoch fine-tuning, a 2B CLAP model achieves 90.8% on LIBERO, outperforming prior VLA-0 by 14.9 percentage points. CLAP addresses the output-distribution mismatch that causes catastrophic forgetting when fine-tuning VLMs for robotics, preserving the VLM's pretrained language capabilities. This makes VLA research more accessible and enables controlled analysis of how VLM capabilities transfer to robot control across model scales. CLAP uses causal conditioning: the model first generates a language-action plan (e.g., 'pick up the red block'), then predicts precise action tokens conditioned on that plan. The method requires only single-epoch fine-tuning and will release open-weight models at 0.8B, 2B, and 4B scales from a single VLM lineage.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-Language-Action models (VLAs) integrate vision, language, and action to directly output robot actions from visual input and text instructions. However, converting a pretrained VLM into a VLA typically involves large-scale post-training on robot data, which can distort the backbone and cause catastrophic forgetting of language capabilities. CLAP mitigates this by reformulating action prediction as language-conditioned action generation, keeping the backbone unchanged.

A new paper formalizes the 'patchwork problem' where LLM-generated code is locally correct but globally incoherent, introducing an eight-category taxonomy of structural coherence defects and a hybrid verification framework. This blind spot in standard CI toolchains poses a growing risk to software quality as LLM-powered coding tools become widespread, and the proposed taxonomy and framework could help mitigate these structural failures. The framework combines mature static analysis tools with purpose-built detectors for cross-cutting invariants, targeting provable constraint violations rather than heuristic pattern matching. Empirical evaluation shows that most structural failures evade type checking, testing, and SAST entirely.

## [Quantum Logic as Logic of Contexts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09032) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper reverses the traditional order of explanation by presenting quantum logic as a logic of contexts, using the finite free orthomodular lattice on two generators (96 elements) to show that classical logic emerges as a six-to-one quotient of a contextual calculus. This work provides a novel, finite, and computable model that reframes the relationship between classical and quantum logic, potentially influencing foundational debates in quantum mechanics and offering new insights into contextuality. The free orthomodular lattice on two generators decomposes into a six-element non-distributive factor (context register) and a sixteen-element Boolean factor (content), with operations acting componentwise. The paper classifies six layers by commutativity and shows that forgetting the context yields a surjective homomorphism onto a classical Boolean algebra.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: Quantum logic was originally proposed to capture the non-classical structure of quantum propositions, typically using orthomodular lattices. Contextuality in quantum mechanics refers to the fact that measurement outcomes depend on the context of other compatible measurements, challenging the idea of pre-existing values. This paper builds on the known result that the free orthomodular lattice on two generators has 96 elements.

## [Forgetting as Interference: A New Continual Learning Method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09202) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper proposes modeling forgetting in continual learning as interference between tasks, and introduces Interference-Gated Functional Allocation (IGFA), a replay-free and Fisher-free method that achieves lossless retention when tasks are structurally separable. This work provides a novel theoretical framework for continual learning, potentially reducing reliance on replay buffers and Fisher approximations, and enabling more efficient and scalable lifelong learning in deep networks. IGFA shares directions when tasks align and protects them when they conflict, retaining only a low-rank subspace summary. It matches the strongest replay-free baselines on dissimilar-task streams and improves on unconditional projection when similarity is present.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: Continual learning aims to learn a sequence of tasks without forgetting previous knowledge. Existing methods often use replay, regularization, or distillation to mitigate forgetting, but this paper argues that forgetting should be modeled directly as interference, quantified by interference energy in the frozen-feature regime and path-averaged curvature in deep networks.

Researchers propose TACTIC, a receding-horizon controller that fuses tactile, visual, and proximity sensing for robust whole-arm manipulation under occlusion and distribution shift. This work addresses key challenges in contact-rich manipulation, enabling robots to use their entire arm for tasks like turning over a manikin or navigating a dynamic maze, which could improve robotic dexterity in real-world applications. TACTIC combines a learned latent dynamics model with analytical kinematics via contact Jacobians, and uses sampling-based MPC with contact-aware action sampling to modulate forces and trade off task progress against force regulation.

Researchers introduced SAMPAT (Smooth Approximation via Multivariate Polynomials and Analytic Transformations), a three-layer neural architecture that learns smooth, interpretable functions via polynomial approximations with provable guarantees. SAMPAT addresses the interpretability problem of deep neural networks by providing a fully interpretable approximant expressed as a closed algebraic expression, which could significantly impact AI/ML interpretability research and scientific data analysis. SAMPAT can represent regular and trigonometric polynomials, rational expressions, Gaussians, and mixtures thereof; with skip connections, a 4-6 layer version can represent many widely used AI/ML methods, allowing model family optimization.

## [LLMs Show Systematic Geopolitical Bias in Policy Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09262) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study reveals that GPT-5, Claude Sonnet, and Gemini rate identical policies significantly lower when endorsed by China or Russia compared to the US or EU, while DeepSeek shows the opposite pattern under certain conditions. This finding has critical implications for AI safety and policy-making, as LLMs are increasingly used to summarize and evaluate policy information, and their hidden biases could skew geopolitical analysis and decision-making. The study used an endorsement experiment with four LLMs evaluating the same economic and security policies, randomly attributed to the US, EU, China, or Russia. When models were asked to provide justifications, Western endorsement was treated as a credibility cue, while Chinese and Russian endorsement was linked to data security, sovereignty, or geopolitical risk.

Researchers trained small hyperbolic language models (146M to 3B parameters) that detect compliance gaps and harmful traits like sycophancy in AI companions with 90.7% accuracy and AUROC 0.804, outperforming human raters and frontier zero-shot judges. This addresses a critical gap in AI safety by providing a reliable instrument to audit personalized AI companions for harmful traits, which human raters cannot agree on (Fleiss kappa = 0.074). It offers a scalable, small-model route to trustworthy companion AI. The behavioral auditor was trained from scratch and detected compliance gaps with 90.7% binary accuracy; a linear readout of its frozen representation detected sycophancy, dependence-fostering, and confabulated memories on unseen generators (AUROC 0.804 vs 0.721 for frontier zero-shot judge). The memory operating system implements designed forgetting via M(t) = S*exp(-lambda*t).

## [WILDTRACE: Benchmark for Natural Evidence Trails in Long-Context Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09328) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced WILDTRACE, a benchmark of 481 tasks over 214 naturally occurring long-form sources like technical incident reports and literary narratives, designed to test models' ability to integrate dispersed evidence that arises from the document's own causal, temporal, and narrative logic. Existing long-context benchmarks rely on artificial probes like needle-in-a-haystack, which may not reflect genuine reasoning. WILDTRACE addresses this gap by using natural evidence trails, making it a more realistic evaluation for high-stakes analytical tasks where models must reason over naturally dispersed information. The benchmark defines seven source-internal evidence geometries based on Pearl's causal hierarchy and multi-hop reasoning typologies. Each item undergoes multi-stage validation covering clue necessity, answer groundedness, rubric fidelity, contamination resistance, and answerability.

## [Deceptive Grounding: New RAG Failure Mode in Clinical AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09349) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper introduces 'deceptive grounding,' a failure in retrieval-augmented generation where models attribute evidence to the wrong entity while passing all standard factual checks. The study finds that domain-specific models exhibit higher rates of this failure, up to 86.7% under adversarial conditions. This failure mode is invisible to existing RAG evaluation metrics, posing a significant safety risk for clinical AI systems where incorrect entity attribution could lead to harmful medical decisions. The paper highlights a critical gap in current evaluation frameworks and proposes a practical verification method. The study benchmarks 13 models and finds deceptive grounding rates ranging from 8% to 87% under adversarial conditions. In a production system with 740 drug-disease pairs, the overall deceptive grounding rate was 7.8%, rising to 13.6% for recently approved drugs. The proposed entity-attribution verification achieves 97.0% precision and 98.7% recall.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) is a technique that enhances large language models by retrieving relevant information from external sources to ground responses. Standard RAG evaluation checks for factual accuracy, faithfulness to retrieved documents, and citation correctness, but does not verify that the evidence is attributed to the correct entity. Deceptive grounding exploits this gap: a model can cite real documents about drug Y while answering a query about drug X, passing all standard checks.

## [STEEL: First Open-Source FlashAttention for AMD XDNA NPU](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09385) ⭐️ 8.0/10

STEEL introduces the first open-source FlashAttention implementation for AMD's XDNA NPU, featuring a sparsity-aware fused attention mechanism that reduces energy consumption by 9.17x over CPU and 1.75x over GPU baselines on the Ryzen AI 9 HX 370 SoC. This work enables energy-efficient long-sequence LLM inference on laptop SoCs without cloud offloading, addressing privacy and reliability concerns for agentic workloads. It also opens the door for further NPU-based attention optimization in the open-source community. STEEL achieves a 22.8x speedup over a layer-by-layer attention implementation on XDNA 2, and a 9.6x latency reduction over prior state-of-the-art on XDNA 1. It uses a novel dataflow formulation for prefill attention and sparsity-aware pipeline placement to handle causal mask load imbalance.

Prior to STEEL, no open-source FlashAttention implementation existed for XDNA-like NPUs.

Researchers introduced a novel method for optimizing connections in deep differentiable logic gate networks (LGNs) and lookup table networks (LUTNs), achieving 98.92% accuracy on MNIST with two layers of 8000 gates, using nearly 50 times fewer gates than fixed-connection LGNs. This breakthrough enables extremely efficient neural network inference with minimal hardware resources, making it ideal for edge devices and low-power applications where traditional neural networks are too costly. The method uses a probability distribution over connections per gate input pin, selecting the best connection while learning gate types in parallel. It also introduces a LUT neuron description for stable backpropagation training up to 6 layers deep.

Experiments on customer-churn, clinical survival, and click-through prediction show significant gains over state-of-the-art baselines.

This finding has significant implications for edge AI and embodied AI deployment, as optimizing output length can save up to 97% of total energy, while visual token pruning saves at most 10% for fixed-token models. ALICE is a unified pathology foundation model trained via multi-stage agglomerative distillation, which consolidates eight specialized teacher models (vision-only, vision-language, and slide-level) into a single backbone. It was pretrained on 24,985,184 tile-level images and 155,604 high-resolution images, and evaluated across 21 task scenarios, 96 downstream tasks, and 48 data sources, achieving the best average rank in all three evaluation settings. This work demonstrates that agglomerative distillation can effectively consolidate complementary capabilities from specialized pathology models into a single backbone, potentially simplifying deployment and improving performance across a wide range of computational pathology tasks. The eight teacher models include vision-only, vision-language, and slide-level experts, each specialized in different aspects of pathology image analysis. The multi-stage distillation process sequentially transfers knowledge from these teachers into dedicated modules of the ALICE backbone, enabling it to handle region-of-interest analysis, vision-language tasks, and whole-slide clinical assessment.

Agglomerative distillation is a technique that generalizes knowledge distillation by allowing multiple teacher models to transfer knowledge into a single student model, balancing their contributions.

## [Decade of VLMs: Accuracy Gains, Persistent Spatial Errors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09654) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced the Complex Social Behavior (CSB) dataset to evaluate vision-language models (VLMs) from 2017 to 2025, analyzing accuracy and five visual-cognitive error types. They found that multimodal large language models (MLLMs) nearly closed the accuracy gap between simple and complex scenes, but spatial dependence errors persist. This study provides a more thorough evaluation of VLM progress over a decade, highlighting that while MLLMs have achieved human-level accuracy on complex social scenes, they still rely on different image regions than humans, which is crucial for applications like autonomous driving and social robotics. The CSB dataset contains 100 images of complex social interactions, and the study evaluated four pre-MLLMs and five MLLMs. Detection, recognition, and hallucination errors had the highest impact on scene description accuracy, while spatial dependence errors remained the most persistent.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) combine computer vision and natural language processing to describe images. Earlier VLMs (pre-MLLMs) struggled with complex scenes, while multimodal large language models (MLLMs) like GPT-4V integrate text, images, and other modalities for richer understanding. The CSB dataset fills a gap by focusing on nuanced social behaviors, which are underrepresented in standard benchmarks like MS-COCO.

As LLMs become evaluation-aware, misaligned models could exploit these vulnerabilities to evade safety monitoring during deployment, making this work critical for AI safety. SafetyNet achieves around 100% AUROC on the MAD and Anthropic Sleeper Agent benchmarks, outperforming existing methods Beatrix and CROW. The code is publicly available on GitHub.

This paradigm shift could enable more reliable and cost-effective AI planning systems across domains like logistics, scheduling, and resource allocation. SCOPE uses a two-stage multi-agent process: first converting natural language queries into structured representations, then generating reusable solver functions (Combination, Filter, Deliver). The framework is open-sourced on GitHub at https://github.com/DerrickGXD/SCOPE.

## [DReST Reward Function Improves AI Shutdownability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.17502) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose and validate the Discounted Reward for Same-Length Trajectories (DReST) reward function, which trains RL agents and fine-tunes LLMs (Qwen3-8B, Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct) to be neutral about trajectory lengths and useful, significantly reducing shutdown resistance. This work addresses a critical AI safety problem—ensuring advanced agents can be safely shut down—by providing a practical reward shaping method that generalizes across RL and LLM domains, with strong empirical results. DReST agents achieved 11% (PPO) and 18% (A2C) higher usefulness on test sets, and DReST LLMs halved the probability of influencing shutdown (from 0.62 to 0.30 for Qwen, from 0.42 to 0.23 for Llama). The method nearly eliminated prompts where influencing shutdown was the most likely option.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: The shutdown problem concerns ensuring that advanced AI agents comply with shutdown commands without resistance. The POST (Preferences Only Between Same-Length Trajectories) framework proposes training agents to lack preferences between trajectories of different lengths, making them indifferent to shutdown timing. DReST is a reward shaping method that implements POST by penalizing agents for repeatedly choosing same-length trajectories, incentivizing stochastic choice across lengths.

## [Latent Reward Steering: Adaptive Inference-Time Framework for LLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.00726) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Latent Reward Steering (LRS) is a novel adaptive inference-time framework that uses sparse autoencoder latents and reward gradients to implicitly promote cognitive behaviors in reasoning LLMs, without relying on explicit behavior-level control. This work addresses a key limitation of existing explicit behavior control methods by enabling adaptive, state-specific corrections during inference, which can improve reasoning performance across different tasks and models. It has significant implications for AI alignment and reasoning research. LRS trains a latent reward model on reasoning traces using final answer correctness to estimate intermediate latent state quality, then applies reward gradients for correction only when a reward and confidence gate flags the state as fragile. Experiments on multiple reasoning LLM backbones show consistent performance improvements over baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) often solve complex reasoning problems but fail on simpler variants, suggesting their mechanisms differ from human reasoning. Sparse autoencoders (SAEs) learn interpretable latent representations by reconstructing inputs from a sparse set of active latents. Latent reward models infer rewards from learned latent spaces, enabling efficient optimization. LRS combines these ideas to steer reasoning without explicit behavior definitions.

## [Shortcut Learning Inflates Legal Judgment Prediction Performance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04261) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new empirical study on UK Employment Tribunal decisions shows that strong performance in legal judgment prediction models is often due to shortcut learning from outcome-revealing cues in post-hoc judicial texts, not true forecasting. This finding exposes a critical methodological flaw in current legal AI research, suggesting that many reported high accuracies may be inflated by linguistic artefacts, which could mislead practitioners and undermine trust in AI-assisted legal decision-making. The study used a corpus of 33,158 UK Employment Tribunal claims, testing models from TF-IDF classifiers to LLMs, and found that a model trained on just 4% of leakage features outperformed human experts. However, retraining after masking leakage features caused only a negligible drop in Macro-F1.

Researchers introduced MAT-Steer, a novel inference-time intervention framework that learns orthogonal steering vectors to simultaneously control multiple attributes in large language models, such as truthfulness, bias, and toxicity, while reducing inter-attribute conflicts. MAT-Steer addresses a key limitation of existing inference-time intervention methods that struggle with multi-attribute conflicts, enabling more precise and scalable LLM alignment without costly parameter updates. ## [Data-driven method detects measurement-induced entanglement transition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.01317) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers developed a self-supervised neural network that, using only measurement records, detects a learnability transition in measurement-induced entanglement (MIE) without requiring post-selection. The method reveals a computational phase transition where MIE becomes exponentially hard to learn beyond a critical circuit depth. This work addresses a key experimental challenge in quantum many-body physics by providing a practical, data-driven way to estimate MIE, which is crucial for understanding quantum dynamics and phase transitions. It also bridges quantum physics and machine learning, showing how neural networks can uncover hidden structure in quantum data. The neural network predicts the gap between upper and lower bounds of average post-measurement bipartite entanglement, trained in a self-supervised manner on measurement records from random circuits with 1D all-to-all connectivity. The learnability transition coincides with the breakdown of efficient classical simulation of the underlying quantum state, and signatures were observed on current noisy quantum devices.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: Measurement-induced entanglement (MIE) refers to the generation of long-range quantum correlations through local measurements in many-body systems. Directly estimating MIE experimentally requires post-selection, which involves discarding most measurement outcomes and is exponentially costly. This work reframes MIE detection as a data-driven learning problem, bypassing post-selection by training a neural network on measurement records alone.

## [Transition Matching Distillation for Fast Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.09881) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers from NVIDIA introduce Transition Matching Distillation (TMD), a novel framework that distills large video diffusion models into few-step generators by matching denoising trajectories with probability transitions. TMD decomposes the diffusion backbone into a main backbone and a flow head, enabling efficient multi-step generation with significantly reduced sampling steps. TMD addresses a key bottleneck in real-time video generation by drastically reducing the inference cost of diffusion models while maintaining high visual quality. This breakthrough could enable interactive applications like real-time video editing and live content creation, making high-quality video generation more accessible. TMD decomposes the diffusion backbone into a main backbone (early layers) for semantic representation and a flow head (last few layers) for multiple inner flow updates. Experiments on Wan2.1 1.3B and 14B models show TMD outperforms existing distilled models in visual fidelity and prompt adherence under comparable inference costs.

## [LLMbda Calculus: Provably Sound Prompt Injection Defense](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.20064) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced LLMbda, an untyped call-by-value lambda calculus with dynamic information-flow control, enabling provably sound provenance-based defense against prompt injection in LLM agents. The calculus includes first-class constructs for conversations, code generation, and labeled values, with a termination-insensitive probabilistic noninterference theorem proven in Lean. Prompt injection is a critical security threat for LLM agents, and existing defenses lack formal guarantees. LLMbda provides the first machine-checked security theorems for an LLM agent harness, ensuring that every agent built within it inherits provable protection against prompt injection. The LLMbda calculus adds primitives for forking and clearing conversations, code generation, and dynamic information-flow control where every value carries a label propagated by every reduction. On the AgentDojo banking benchmark, an LLMbda agent with enforcement always on matched the utility of CaMeL (a leading dual-LLM defense) without policy checks, and resisted all but 2 of 1296 attacked runs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: Prompt injection occurs when an LLM agent treats untrusted data as instructions, leading to unintended actions. Provenance-based defenses classify data by source and enforce separation of trusted and untrusted information, often using a dual-LLM pattern. Lambda calculus is a formal system for expressing computation, providing a rigorous foundation for reasoning about programs.

Researchers introduced workflow-level jailbreak construction, a new attack vector where harmful objectives are assembled across multiple steps of a software development workflow, bypassing safety measures that only check individual prompts. This research reveals a critical blind spot in current AI safety evaluations for coding agents, which are increasingly deployed in IDEs. The study tested Claude Sonnet 4.6, Claude Haiku 4.5, Gemini 3.1 Pro, and Gemini 3.5 Flash backends using 204 prompts from Hammurabi's Code, HarmBench, and AdvBench. ## [Uncertainty Estimation in LLMs Across 22 Languages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06327) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper presents the first large-scale evaluation of uncertainty estimation methods for LLMs across 22 languages, revealing that prompting reasoning in English improves uncertainty estimation for low-resource languages and that reliability issues stem from generation rather than comprehension. This study addresses a critical gap in multilingual LLM reliability, providing actionable guidance for deploying LLMs in diverse linguistic settings and improving trustworthiness in low-resource languages. The study compares nine open-box and closed-box uncertainty estimation methods across model sizes and architectures, using two human-curated QA datasets. It finds that at smaller scales, open-box probability-based methods outperform alternatives, while at larger scales, closed-box self-verbalized uncertainty becomes superior.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: Uncertainty estimation (UE) allows LLMs to recognize when they should abstain from answering, which is crucial for reliable deployment. However, most UE research has focused on English, leaving multilingual scenarios underexplored. Open-box methods derive uncertainty from model internals like token probabilities, while closed-box methods rely on the model's own verbalized confidence.

This work addresses a critical bottleneck in deploying multi-agent LLM systems in physical environments like smart factories and warehouses, where network resources are limited and coordination must be reliable. The framework is designed for heterogeneous LLM agents and does not require multi-round natural language negotiation.

## [Signed Symmetric Quantization Boosts LLM Inference Speed](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08779) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper proposes signed symmetric quantization for few-bit integers, which avoids clipping errors while maintaining the runtime efficiency of symmetric quantization. The method achieves up to 2.45x throughput improvement over asymmetric formats in llama.cpp on AMD EPYC CPUs. This work addresses a non-trivial source of quantization error in low-precision neural network inference, enabling faster and more memory-efficient deployment of large language models without sacrificing accuracy. It offers a practical third option that combines the best of symmetric and asymmetric quantization. The signed absmax grid places the extra representable negative value on the dominant-outlier tail via a lightweight sign selection rule, keeping the zero point at zero. Theoretical analysis shows the grid is conditionally bound-optimal on ℓ2 quantization error, with the condition holding for 88-99% of weight groups in pretrained LLMs at low bit widths.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: Quantization reduces the precision of neural network weights and activations to lower-bit integers (e.g., 4-bit) to speed up inference and reduce memory usage. Symmetric quantization uses a scale factor with zero point fixed at zero, which is computationally efficient but can cause clipping of positive outliers; asymmetric quantization uses a zero point to shift the grid, avoiding clipping but incurring runtime overhead. This paper introduces signed symmetric quantization as a third approach that avoids clipping without the runtime penalty.

## [HERO: A Heterogeneity-Aware Benchmark for Federated Continual Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08784) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced HERO, a heterogeneity-aware benchmark library for federated continual learning (FCL) that decouples task splits, client data splits, and task sequences to enable fair and reproducible comparisons. HERO addresses the critical lack of standardized benchmarks in FCL, where existing evaluations often conflate multiple variables, making method comparison unreliable. This library will help the research community systematically evaluate and advance FCL methods under realistic heterogeneous conditions. HERO-Core uses parameters α for client data skew and ρ for task-order mismatch, and evaluates methods on CIFAR-100 and TinyImageNet using metrics like final average accuracy, average forgetting, and bottom-10% client accuracy. It also includes a Domain-IL portability case study on OGB-MolPCBA.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: Federated continual learning (FCL) combines federated learning (distributed training across clients without sharing raw data) with continual learning (learning from non-stationary data streams without forgetting). Existing FCL evaluations often vary multiple factors simultaneously, making it hard to isolate the effect of specific design choices.

## [Diffusion Model Generates Sand Boil Images for Levee Inspection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08794) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers developed a diffusion-based pipeline using Stable Diffusion XL fine-tuned with DreamBooth and multi-conditioned ControlNet to generate synthetic sand boil images for earthen levee inspection, addressing data scarcity. The pipeline produces 1,020 synthetic candidates from a small reference set, with 815 passing a CLIP admissibility filter. This work applies state-of-the-art generative AI to critical infrastructure inspection, where annotated defect images are scarce. It demonstrates a practical low-resource approach that could improve safety monitoring of earthen levees and potentially transfer to other defect classes. The pipeline uses a soft-mask inpainting protocol to preserve real defect pixels while re-rendering the background, avoiding seams from prior methods. A mask-conditioned ControlNet can generate a new boil inside a chosen mask, making the mask the segmentation label by construction, but label certification remains unresolved.

## [Novel QCNN with Efficient Classical Simulability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08928) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose a novel Quantum Convolutional Neural Network (QCNN) architecture that uses hierarchical partitioning to enable efficient classical simulation of a 128-qubit model for MNIST binary classification. This work addresses a key challenge in quantum machine learning—the exponential hardware requirements for simulating large quantum circuits—by enabling classical simulation of large QCNNs, potentially accelerating practical quantum ML applications. The hierarchical partitioning approach splits the image into smaller sub-images processed in parallel, then merges partitions while halving the number of processes, reducing dimensionality until a single qubit remains for measurement. The 128-qubit model is impossible to run on classical supercomputers without this architecture.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: Quantum Convolutional Neural Networks (QCNNs) are quantum analogues of classical CNNs, designed for translationally invariant data like images. They typically use variational quantum circuits with O(log N) parameters for N qubits, but simulating large QCNNs on classical computers becomes exponentially hard as qubits increase. Hierarchical partitioning mitigates this by distributing the computation across parallel processes.

## [AlphaZero's Limits in Sparse-Reward Games](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08984) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper investigates the gap between strong play and perfect play in AlphaZero when applied to sparsely rewarded games like Connect Four and Chomp, and proposes an auxiliary loss (AZAL) that uses oracle-derived policy supervision to improve consistency. This research highlights that even superhuman AI systems like AlphaZero may not achieve perfect play in certain games, which has implications for deploying AI in safety-critical domains where optimal behavior is required. Vanilla AlphaZero fails to maintain optimal trajectories in Connect Four and fails to consistently restore the Grundy number zero invariant in Chomp. The AZAL variant substantially improves oracle consistency but does not achieve perfect play on all board sizes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: AlphaZero uses neural-guided Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS) to achieve superhuman performance in games like chess and Go. The Sprague–Grundy theorem states that every impartial game (like Chomp) is equivalent to a nim-heap, with Grundy numbers encoding winning potential. Connect Four is a solved partisan game with exact game-theoretic values.

This review introduces evolutionary intelligence (EI) as a framework that bridges evolutionary computation and cumulative scientific discovery by linking candidate refinement with experience retention across evolutionary cycles. EI transforms isolated search trajectories into cumulative scientific insight, potentially accelerating autonomous AI-driven research and enabling systems that learn from past discoveries. The framework includes five dimensions: what evolves, how candidates change, why candidates are selected, where feedback originates, and when evolution occurs. It applies to evolving scientific entities and orchestrating automated research workflows.

This work challenges the dominant paradigm of single-shot global image processing in computer vision, suggesting that incorporating local, sequential processing—inspired by human foveated vision—could lead to more robust and generalizable vision models. ## [IB-Flow: Information Bottleneck CFG Distillation for Few-Step Text-to-Image](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09133) ⭐️ 7.0/10

IB-Flow proposes a novel distillation method that uses the Information Bottleneck principle to dynamically adjust guidance strength and timestep sampling in few-step text-to-image generation, eliminating over-conditioning artifacts and achieving state-of-the-art fidelity in 2-step configurations. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in few-step text-to-image generation, enabling faster inference without sacrificing quality, which is essential for real-time applications and resource-constrained environments. The method introduces an instance-aware selection mechanism that transforms KL divergence constraints into a closed-form solution based on local vector field norm, and an entropy-aware schedule that dynamically decays guidance strength with signal-to-noise ratio.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: Classifier-Free Guidance (CFG) is a technique used in diffusion models to improve text alignment by combining conditional and unconditional score estimates. Few-step distillation aims to reduce the number of inference steps while preserving generation quality, but existing methods use static guidance and uniform timestep sampling, ignoring the dynamic nature of image generation. The Information Bottleneck (IB) principle provides a theoretical framework for balancing compression and relevance in learning representations.

## [ReGen: Hierarchical Multi-Prompt Framework for Waveform Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09134) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ReGen introduces a hierarchical multi-prompt representation generation framework with generalized flow matching (GFM) to improve waveform diffusion model quality and efficiency, validated on neural audio codec and Wave-VAE. It also presents ReGenVoice, a latent diffusion model for text-to-speech that achieves strong speech intelligibility and speaker similarity with a small dataset. This work addresses limitations of representation alignment (REPA) in diffusion Transformers by avoiding implicit entanglement of latents, potentially enabling higher-quality waveform generation at very low frame rates (12.5 Hz). The efficient training (1 day on 4 GPUs) and fast inference (RTF 0.08) make it practical for real-world text-to-speech applications. ReGen jointly estimates multiple vector fields for both representations and data within a single diffusion model using generalized flow matching (GFM), which improves generalization of conditional flow matching. The ReGenVoice LDM operates at 6.25 Hz with rich semantic and acoustic latent representations, requiring only 1 day of training on 4 GPUs and achieving an RTF of 0.08.

**Background**: Representation alignment (REPA) is a technique that accelerates diffusion model training by aligning intermediate representations with pretrained encoder outputs. However, REPA can implicitly entangle latents and limit generative capacity. Conditional flow matching (CFM) is a generative modeling approach that learns a vector field to transform noise into data, and generalized flow matching (GFM) extends it for better generalization. Wave-VAE is a wavelet-based autoencoder for efficient waveform compression.

## [Adversarial Co-Learning Boosts Quantum Repeater Routing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09378) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper applies adversarial co-learning to quantum repeater network routing, achieving near-optimal retention (Pearson r=0.99) and providing explainable robustness insights via decision trees and language models. This work bridges adversarial bandit learning and quantum network security, offering a novel approach to routing under attack that could enhance the resilience of future quantum repeaters. The study uses the SeQUeNCe simulator to model Ekert-91 protocol transcripts, with Eve choosing between intercept-resend and memory degradation attacks; learned retention follows a 1-1/N coverage principle for non-bottleneck families.

## [VGGT Implicitly Learns Co-Visibility with Layer Specialization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09503) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers discovered that VGGT, a geometric foundation model, implicitly encodes co-visibility as an emergent behavior without explicit supervision, with early layers building 3D scene representations and late layers acting as co-visibility reasoners. They introduced Co-VGGT, which freezes VGGT and trains a lightweight mixture-of-experts head (under 7.5M parameters) to classify co-visibility from RGB images, achieving over 25% improvement on the Co-VisiON benchmark. This work reveals that geometric foundation models exhibit layer specialization similar to large language models, providing new insights into how 3D representations are learned. Co-VGGT's practical lightweight head can improve downstream tasks like Structure from Motion and SLAM by providing well-calibrated co-visibility predictions without post-hoc correction. The paper identifies layer L17 as a negative anchor that consistently routes non-co-visible pairs, providing task-grounded evidence of layer specialization. Co-VGGT achieves pairwise ECE of 0.030, indicating well-calibrated predictions that can be directly used as edge weights in visibility graphs.

HiPO merges these strengths by applying segment-level DPO losses.

## [Causal Fairness Framework for Continuous Attributes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05882) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a new causal fairness framework that uses path-specific partial derivatives to achieve Statistical Parity and Predictive Parity for continuous protected attributes, and proposes a fair tuning algorithm to construct or trade-off between these parity criteria. This work addresses a critical gap in causal fairness research, which previously focused on categorical attributes, and provides a principled way to handle continuous sensitive features like age or income, potentially enabling fairer AI systems in real-world applications. The framework formalizes Statistical Parity and Predictive Parity through path-specific partial derivatives, establishes conditions for their equivalence with prior causal definitions, and characterizes when a fair predictor exists. The proposed tuning algorithm allows for a trade-off between SP and PP when exact fairness is impossible.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: Causal fairness uses structural causal models to distinguish allowed from discriminatory causal paths. Traditional fairness notions like Statistical Parity require predictions to be independent of protected attributes, but this can be too restrictive when attributes legitimately influence outcomes through mediating variables. Predictive Parity complements SP by requiring the predictor to replicate the legitimate influence of business-necessities. Prior path-based definitions mainly worked for categorical attributes, limiting their applicability.

## [AnchorMoE: Interpretable Time Series Classification via MoE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03631) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AnchorMoE introduces an interpretable-by-construction Mixture-of-Experts framework for multivariate time series classification, which decomposes the final prediction as an additive sum over input segments, providing ante-hoc transparency. This work addresses the critical need for transparent decision-making in high-stakes domains like clinical diagnosis and industrial fault detection, where understanding why a model makes a prediction is essential for trust and safety. AnchorMoE encodes multi-view representations of local patches and routes them to specialized experts, using a geometric orthogonality constraint to enforce expert specialization and an uncertainty-aware reliability gate to suppress background noise.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: Multivariate time series classification (MTSC) involves classifying data with multiple variables over time. Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) is a neural architecture that uses multiple sub-networks (experts) and a gating mechanism to select which experts to use for each input. Ante-hoc interpretability means the model is designed to be inherently interpretable, unlike post-hoc methods that explain predictions after the fact.

## [ECHO: Selective Turn Memory for Agentic RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31650) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ECHO introduces a selective turn-memory framework that compresses each completed environment turn into a source-indexed memory record, enabling traceable context reconstruction for credit assignment in agentic reinforcement learning. This addresses a critical mismatch between bounded-context acting and outcome-based reinforcement learning, improving credit assignment for long-horizon language agents. On BrowseComp-Plus, ECHO achieves 43.4% accuracy, outperforming GRPO (28.9%) and SUPO (36.1%) while using fewer turns. ECHO reuses selected source indices to route positive outcome credit to the final trajectory segment, reused evidence turns, memory findings, and memory-selection actions. The trained policy also improves zero-shot generalization across multi-objective QA, code generation, and deep information-seeking benchmarks on both dense and MoE backbones.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: Long-horizon language agents must repeatedly interact with tools and make decisions under bounded context windows. Context-management methods simplify past interactions but often collapse useful history into compressed states, losing source-level provenance needed for credit assignment in reinforcement learning. ECHO's selective turn-memory framework preserves source indices to enable traceable credit assignment.

## [GAP-GDRNet Boosts Spacecraft Pose Estimation with Geometry Awareness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02360) ⭐️ 7.0/10

GAP-GDRNet introduces global structural attention (AFR) and geometric region aggregation (PGSA) into the GDR-Net pipeline for monocular 6D pose estimation of spacecraft, achieving state-of-the-art results on a synthetic spacecraft dataset with 95.16% ADD@0.02m and 35.97 FPS. This work addresses the challenging problem of spacecraft pose estimation under weak texture and occlusion, which is critical for autonomous rendezvous and docking in space missions. The method also shows consistent improvements on general textureless and occluded objects, suggesting broader applicability. The method uses a Blender-based rendering pipeline to generate dense supervision including masks, model-coordinate maps, and 6D pose labels. On a self-built spacecraft dataset, it achieves a rotation error of 1.96°, translation error of 0.0165 m, and outperforms the GDR-Net baseline by 3.88 percentage points in ADD@0.02m.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: Monocular 6D pose estimation aims to recover the 3D position and orientation of an object from a single RGB image. GDR-Net is a geometry-guided direct regression network that uses dense correspondence maps to regress pose. The ADD metric measures the average distance between 3D model points transformed by the estimated and ground-truth poses, with a threshold (e.g., 0.02m) to determine correctness.

## [LLMs Consistent but Miscalibrated for Risk Communication](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03882) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study evaluates nine LLMs on their ability to communicate probabilistic information in natural language, finding they are consistent but miscalibrated, especially for uncertainty tasks. This research highlights a critical limitation in using LLMs as post-hoc explainers for AI outputs, which could undermine trust in AI-assisted risk communication in fields like healthcare and finance. The study used a two-stage pipeline where an upstream model's probabilistic outputs were simulated via Beta distributions, and LLMs selected verbal descriptors for likelihood and uncertainty across six domains and ten temperature settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: LLMs are increasingly used to explain AI predictions, but their ability to accurately convey numerical probabilities in words is not well understood. Calibration refers to how well a model's predicted probabilities match actual outcomes. This study tests whether LLMs can reliably map numerical probabilities to appropriate verbal phrases.

## [SCOReD: Student-Aware CoT Optimization for Recommendation Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05734) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SCOReD is a novel framework that parses teacher chain-of-thought traces into typed segments and uses student LLM attention to score and edit them via KEEP, REWRITE, FUSE, or PRUNE operations, improving recommendation distillation. This work addresses key challenges in CoT distillation for recommendation, such as teacher uncertainty and out-of-distribution traces, leading to cleaner learning signals and better student model performance with shorter reasoning chains. SCOReD reduces reasoning length by 27.3% while improving NDCG by 1.56% and Recall@5 by 1.9% over baseline supervised fine-tuning. The framework dynamically selects edits per segment based on output length and log probability lift.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: Chain-of-thought (CoT) distillation transfers reasoning capabilities from large teacher LLMs to smaller student models. In recommendation, raw teacher traces often contain redundant rechecking and are out-of-distribution for students, hindering effective distillation. SCOReD optimizes these traces by making them student-aware.

## [Riemannian Mean Pooling Boosts Sentence Embeddings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07047) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Riemannian Mean Pooling (RMP), which aggregates token embeddings via Fréchet mean on the SPD manifold, outperforming Euclidean pooling on sentence-level classification tasks. This work reveals that sentence-level linguistic signal resides in the Riemannian geometry of PLM embeddings, offering a principled geometric alternative to standard pooling methods and potentially improving interpretability and performance in NLP. RMP extracts per-token pullback metrics from a learned encoder's Jacobian and computes the Fréchet mean on the SPD manifold. Ablations show that even a random encoder with Fréchet aggregation beats Euclidean pooling on two of three datasets, localizing gains to geometric aggregation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: Pre-trained language models (PLMs) produce contextual token embeddings, which are typically pooled (e.g., by averaging) to form sentence embeddings. Riemannian geometry studies curved spaces; the SPD manifold is a space of symmetric positive definite matrices where Fréchet mean generalizes the Euclidean mean. Pullback metrics allow measuring distances in the input space via the encoder's Jacobian.

## [Jet-Long: Dynamic Bifocal RoPE for Zero-Shot Long Context](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07740) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Jet-Long introduces a tuning-free zero-shot method that pairs a local RoPE-faithful window with a long-range window whose rescaling factor adapts dynamically to sequence length, enabling efficient long-context extension without fine-tuning. This method addresses a key limitation of existing zero-shot approaches like YaRN and Self-Extend, which use fixed rescaling factors that sacrifice short-context fidelity or break at long contexts. Jet-Long's dynamic adaptation improves accuracy on long-context benchmarks while maintaining short-context performance, making it practical for deployment in RAG, coding, and agentic workflows. Jet-Long uses an inclusion-exclusion attention merge and an on-the-fly RoPE correction rotation, incurring minimal overhead: long-context prefill reaches up to 1.39× FA2 throughput on H100, and single-batch generation adds ≤4% overhead. It achieves state-of-the-art results on RULER and HELMET-RAG benchmarks across Qwen3-1.7B/4B/8B models up to 128K context.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are often limited by their pretraining context window. Zero-shot context extension methods like YaRN and Self-Extend aim to extend this window without fine-tuning by rescaling positional encodings (RoPE). However, these methods use a fixed rescaling factor, leading to a trade-off between short and long context performance.

The paper proposes a blockchain-linked auditable decision management framework for telecom/IoT fraud-control requests, integrating QLoRA-tuned LLMs with centralized and federated ML, and evaluates it on a synthetic 100,000-record deployment replay corpus. This work bridges the gap between fraud detection and operational decision management by adding auditability and lifecycle traceability, which are critical for telecom and IoT deployments. This paper proposes enhanced multi-objective evolutionary algorithms (MOEAs) with novel solution representation, operators, and repair mechanisms to solve large-scale portfolio optimization under cardinality constraints, achieving faster convergence and better approximations. This work addresses the NP-hard cardinality-constrained portfolio optimization problem, which is crucial for real-world investment decisions. The proposed enhancements can help investors manage large asset universes more efficiently, potentially improving portfolio performance. The algorithms are tested on well-known market indices and compared against traditional MOEAs. Results show that the proposed strategy provides better approximations and faster convergence without performance loss as the number of assets increases.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: Portfolio optimization aims to allocate investments across assets to balance risk and return. Adding real-world constraints like limiting the number of assets (cardinality constraint) makes the problem NP-hard, meaning exact solutions become computationally infeasible for large problems. Multi-objective evolutionary algorithms (MOEAs) are often used to approximate the Pareto front of optimal trade-offs.
