<a id="item-184"></a>
## [Two-Agent QA System Wins QANTA 2026 with Confidence Calibration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09623) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A task-specific two-agent architecture using GPT-4.1-mini and GPT-4.1 achieved the highest overall leaderboard score of 0.402 in the QANTA 2026 multimodal quizbowl challenge, with a Tossup score of 0.238 and a Bonus Effect score of 0.164. This work demonstrates that lightweight, task-specific reasoning strategies with confidence calibration can outperform more complex retrieval or ensemble methods in resource-constrained multimodal QA, offering a practical blueprint for efficient AI systems. The Tossup agent uses a GPT-4.1-mini model with confidence-calibrated answering and a domain-specific numeric reasoning policy to reduce overconfident predictions from isolated quantitative clues, while the Bonus agent uses GPT-4.1 with leadin-aware reasoning and multimodal evidence integration.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: QANTA 2026 is a shared challenge at the ICML 2026 Workshop on Efficient Multimodal Question Answering (EMM-QA), focusing on pyramid-style quizbowl questions with incrementally revealed text and images. Pyramid-style questions use clues arranged from hardest to easiest, rewarding deeper knowledge. Confidence calibration adjusts a model's output probabilities to better reflect true correctness likelihood.

## [Transformer-Actor-Critic RL for 6G SFC Partitioning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.18902) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper proposes a Transformer-empowered actor-critic reinforcement learning framework for sequence-aware service function chain partitioning in 6G networks, introducing ε-LoPe exploration and Asymptotic Return Normalization. This approach addresses scalability and VNF inter-dependency challenges in SFC partitioning, outperforming existing methods in acceptance rate, resource utilization, and scalability, which is critical for efficient 6G network management. The framework uses self-attention to model VNF inter-dependencies, enabling parallel decision-making. The ε-LoPe strategy and Asymptotic Return Normalization improve training stability and convergence.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: Service Function Chains (SFCs) are ordered sequences of Virtualized Network Functions (VNFs) that deliver network services. Partitioning an SFC across domains is challenging due to heterogeneity and QoS constraints. Actor-critic RL combines policy gradient and value-based methods for efficient learning.

<a id="item-186"></a>
## [Simple Vectors Explain Human Choice in Hide-and-Seek](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.03643) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new paper introduces probability antimatching as the avoidance counterpart of probability matching, modeling human choice in hide-and-seek tasks using simple vector representations. This work provides a parsimonious geometric model that captures diverse human decision strategies in stochastic environments, potentially influencing cognitive science and AI decision-making research. The model uses a linear combination of just two vectors—matching and maximizing for seeking, antimatching and minimizing for hiding—to fit participant choices in up to 7 rooms.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: Probability matching is a well-known bias where people choose options in proportion to their probability of success, rather than always picking the best option. Probability antimatching is its conceptual inversion for avoidance scenarios. This study extends the hide-and-seek paradigm to compare seeking and hiding behaviors.

<a id="item-187"></a>
## [H3Former: Hypergraph-Based FGVC with Hyperbolic Contrastive Loss](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.10260) ⭐️ 6.0/10

H3Former introduces a token-to-region framework that uses a Semantic-Aware Aggregation Module (SAAM) with hypergraph convolution to capture high-order semantic relations, and a Hyperbolic Hierarchical Contrastive Loss (HHCL) to enforce hierarchical constraints in non-Euclidean space for fine-grained visual classification. This work addresses the challenge of capturing discriminative cues in fine-grained visual classification by modeling high-order semantic dependencies, potentially improving performance on tasks like bird species or car model recognition where subtle differences matter. The SAAM dynamically constructs a weighted hypergraph among tokens and applies hypergraph convolution to aggregate token features into region-level representations, while HHCL operates in hyperbolic space to preserve hierarchical category relationships.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 13, 04:00

**Background**: Fine-grained visual classification (FGVC) aims to distinguish between subcategories with high similarity, such as different bird species or car models. Traditional methods often use feature selection or region proposal strategies but may introduce redundancy or miss subtle cues. Hypergraphs extend standard graphs by allowing edges to connect more than two nodes, enabling modeling of high-order relationships. Hyperbolic space is well-suited for hierarchical data due to its exponential volume growth.
